It's not just the mess itself, it's what happens because of it. Um, the shop has had late orders, and some confused billing lately because I lose track of things. That's led to some pretty tense moments with the other staff, and with the customers, which feels awful. \n\nAnd it's affecting me outside of work, too. At my church, I've actually had to step back from leading some of the planning we do... people were just getting frustrated with me because I couldn't keep it all straight. So, yes, it's been very upsetting.\n\nDoctor: Have you always had trouble with organization, or is this a relatively new issue for you?\n\nPatient: Well, it hasn't always been this bad. It's been causing real trouble for about two years and two months now. That's right around the time I started changing the business into this more complicated boutique model. Before that, I managed okay, but with all the new details and how things are set up now, it's just really gotten away from me.\n\nDoctor: Are you experiencing any difficulties in your personal relationships with family or friends due to your current situation?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, my family and friends seem fine with me. It's really just the people at work and the church committee who have been getting frustrated. My personal relationships at home haven't really been affected by this.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble remembering things that you would normally be able to recall?\n\nPatient: No, not really. My memory itself seems fine. I mean, I don't forget things I already know or anything like that. It's more just... losing track of the physical papers or the schedules for the shop, like I was saying. But as far as just remembering normal everyday things, no, I haven't noticed any problems with that.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself getting easily distracted while trying to work or focus on tasks?\n\nPatient: Yes, I definitely do. I can start going over the day's dry-cleaning orders just fine, but... after a few minutes, my mind is just off on something else. I'll start thinking about clothing labels, or my choir plans, or just something going on at home. And then I realize I completely missed half of what I was actually supposed to be checking.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I mean, I'm not depressed or anything like that. I get a little annoyed with myself sometimes when I mess up things at the shop, but as far as just feeling sad... no, my mood has been fine.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding certain tasks or situations at work because you feel overwhelmed by them?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really avoid things. I mean, I might put off doing the inventory for a bit because I know I'll get sidetracked, but I don't avoid tasks completely just because they're overwhelming. I still try to get everything done, even if it takes me longer than it should.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I know I get sidetracked, but I don't just... jump into things without thinking. I'm actually pretty careful about what I do, especially at the shop with people's clothes. I don't really make rash decisions or act out of the blue like that.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble managing your daily routines or responsibilities at home because of your difficulties with organization and focus?\n\nPatient: Well, at home it's mostly okay, but it's definitely causing trouble in other areas. But as far as just remembering normal everyday things, no, I haven't noticed any problems with that.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself getting easily distracted while trying to work or focus on tasks?\n\nPatient: Yes, I definitely do. I can start going over the day's dry-cleaning orders just fine, but... after a few minutes, my mind is just off on something else. I'll start thinking about clothing labels, or my choir plans, or just something going on at home. And then I realize I completely missed half of what I was actually supposed to be checking.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I mean, I'm not depressed or anything like that. I get a little annoyed with myself sometimes when I mess up things at the shop, but as far as just feeling sad... no, my mood has been fine.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding certain tasks or situations at work because you feel overwhelmed by them?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really avoid things. I mean, I might put off doing the inventory for a bit because I know I'll get sidetracked, but I don't avoid tasks completely just because they're overwhelming. I still try to get everything done, even if it takes me longer than it should.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I know I get sidetracked, but I don't just... jump into things without thinking. I'm actually pretty careful about what I do, especially at the shop with people's clothes. I don't really make rash decisions or act out of the blue like that.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble managing your daily routines or responsibilities at home because of your difficulties with organization and focus?\n\nPatient: Well, at home it's mostly okay, but it's definitely causing trouble in other areas. Like at the shop, we've had late orders and... some confused billing lately. It's caused a few tense moments, honestly, with the staff and the customers. And at church, I actually had to step back from leading some of the planning stuff. I could tell people were getting frustrated with me. So, yeah, it is making my responsibilities harder to manage.\n\nDoctor: Was there anything significant happening in your life around the time your organizational issues started, about two years and two months ago?\n\nPatient: Yes, actually. That's exactly when I started changing the business into this more complicated boutique model. It's been causing real trouble for me ever since we made that shift, so... yeah, that's been about two years and two months now.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. It's been causing real trouble for about two years and two months now. That's right around the time I started changing the business into this more complicated boutique model. Before that, I managed okay, but with all the new details and how things are set up now, it's just really gotten away from me.\n\nDoctor: Are you experiencing any difficulties in your personal relationships with family or friends due to your current situation?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, my family and friends seem fine with me. It's really just the people at work and the church committee who have been getting frustrated. It's more just... losing track of the physical papers or the schedules for the shop, like I was saying. It just turns into this big pile until I honestly do not know what to handle first. It's really starting to become a problem for me to do my job.\n\nDoctor: How much distress or anxiety are you experiencing because of this disorganization at work?\n\nPatient: It's causing me a lot of stress, honestly. It's not just the mess itself, it's what happens because of it. Um, the shop has had late orders, and some confused billing lately because I lose track of things. That's led to some pretty tense moments with the other staff, and with the customers, which feels awful. \n\nAnd it's affecting me outside of work, too. At my church, I've actually had to step back from leading some of the planning we do... people were just getting frustrated with me because I couldn't keep it all straight. So, yes, it's been very upsetting.\n\nDoctor: Have you always had trouble with organization, or is this a relatively new issue for you?\n\nPatient: Well, it hasn't always been this bad. It's been causing real trouble for about two years and two months now. That's right around the time I started changing the business into this more complicated boutique model. Before that, I managed okay, but with all the new details and how things are set up now, it's just really gotten away from me.\n\nDoctor: Are you experiencing any difficulties in your personal relationships with family or friends due to your current situation?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, my family and friends seem fine with me. It's really just the people at work and the church committee who have been getting frustrated. My personal relationships at home haven't really been affected by this.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble remembering things that you would normally be able to recall?\n\nPatient: No, not really. My memory itself seems fine. I mean, I don't forget things I already know or anything like that. It's more just... losing track of the physical papers or the schedules for the shop, like I was saying. But as far as just remembering normal everyday things, no, I haven't noticed any problems with that.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself getting easily distracted while trying to work or focus on tasks?\n\nPatient: Yes, I definitely do. I can start going over the day's dry-cleaning orders just fine, but... after a few minutes, my mind is just off on something else. I'll start thinking about clothing labels, or my choir plans, or just something going on at home. And then I realize I completely missed half of what I was actually supposed to be checking.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I mean, I'm not depressed or anything like that. I get a little annoyed with myself sometimes when I mess up things at the shop, but as far as just feeling sad... no, my mood has been fine.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding certain tasks or situations at work because you feel overwhelmed by them?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really avoid things. I mean, I might put off doing the inventory for a bit because I know I'll get sidetracked, but I don't avoid tasks completely just because they're overwhelming. I still try to get everything done, even if it takes me longer than it should.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I know I get sidetracked, but I don't just... jump into things without thinking. I'm actually pretty careful about what I do, especially at the shop with people's clothes. I don't really make rash decisions or act out of the blue like that.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble managing your daily routines or responsibilities at home because of your difficulties with organization and focus?\n\nPatient: Well, at home it's mostly okay, but it's definitely causing trouble in other areas. Like at the shop, we've had late orders and... some confused billing lately. It's caused a few tense moments, honestly, with the staff and the customers. And at church, I actually had to step back from leading some of the planning stuff. I could tell people were getting frustrated with me. So, yeah, it is making my responsibilities harder to manage.\n\nDoctor: Was there anything significant happening in your life around the time your organizational issues started, about two years and two months ago?\n\nPatient: Yes, actually. It's been causing real trouble for me ever since we made that shift, so... yeah, that's been about two years and two months now.\n\nDoctor: Do you have physical symptoms like a racing heart, sweating, or trembling when you feel stressed or overwhelmed?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really get anything like that. I mean, I feel stressed out and overwhelmed a lot with the shop and everything, but my heart doesn't race, and I don't get sweaty or shaky. It's more just... mental stress, I guess you'd call it. Not physical.\n\nDoctor: Are you having any difficulty managing your personal care or daily activities at home due to your organizational issues?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Like I mentioned before, things at home are mostly fine. I'm still keeping up with my personal care and all the daily stuff around the house without any real issues. It's just the work and church stuff that's been a problem.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel mentally tired or exhausted after trying to focus on tasks or organize things?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I get tired at the end of the day like anyone else, but it's not like a mental exhaustion just from trying to focus. It's more that I just get frustrated or sidetracked, not necessarily worn out in my head.\n\nDoctor: Are your difficulties with organization and focus at work affecting your overall quality of life?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it really is. At the shop, it's... well, it's causing some real problems. We've had late orders, the billing gets confused, and that just leads to some pretty tense moments with the staff and the customers. So, yes, it's definitely taking a toll on things.\n\nDoctor: Are you noticing any strain in your relationships with friends or family members due to your current situation?\n\nPatient: No, not really. My family and friends have been pretty understanding, honestly. It's mostly just the work relationships and the church folks that have been strained because of the mistakes I've been making. But at home, things are okay.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or criticize yourself for the mistakes you've been making at work or with the church planning?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel guilty or beat myself up over it. I mean, I know I'm making mistakes, and it's frustrating when things go wrong at the shop or when I had to step down at church, but I don't really sit around criticizing myself. It is what it is, and I'm just trying to figure out how to handle it better.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. My personal relationships at home haven't really been affected by this.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble remembering things that you would normally be able to recall?\n\nPatient: No, not really. My memory itself seems fine. I mean, I don't forget things I already know or anything like that. It's more just... losing track of the physical papers or the schedules for the shop, like I was saying. But as far as just remembering normal everyday things, no, I haven't noticed any problems with that.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself getting easily distracted while trying to work or focus on tasks?\n\nPatient: Yes, I definitely do. I can start going over the day's dry-cleaning orders just fine, but... after a few minutes, my mind is just off on something else. I'll start thinking about clothing labels, or my choir plans, or just something going on at home. And then I realize I completely missed half of what I was actually supposed to be checking.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I mean, I'm not depressed or anything like that. I get a little annoyed with myself sometimes when I mess up things at the shop, but as far as just feeling sad... no, my mood has been fine.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding certain tasks or situations at work because you feel overwhelmed by them?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really avoid things. I mean, I might put off doing the inventory for a bit because I know I'll get sidetracked, but I don't avoid tasks completely just because they're overwhelming. I still try to get everything done, even if it takes me longer than it should.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I know I get sidetracked, but I don't just... jump into things without thinking. I'm actually pretty careful about what I do, especially at the shop with people's clothes. I don't really make rash decisions or act out of the blue like that.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble managing your daily routines or responsibilities at home because of your difficulties with organization and focus?\n\nPatient: Well, at home it's mostly okay, but it's definitely causing trouble in other areas. Like at the shop, we've had late orders and... some confused billing lately. It's caused a few tense moments, honestly, with the staff and the customers. And at church, I actually had to step back from leading some of the planning stuff. I could tell people were getting frustrated with me. So, yeah, it is making my responsibilities harder to manage.\n\nDoctor: Was there anything significant happening in your life around the time your organizational issues started, about two years and two months ago?\n\nPatient: Yes, actually. That's exactly when I started changing the business into this more complicated boutique model. It's been causing real trouble for me ever since we made that shift, so... yeah, that's been about two years and two months now.\n\nDoctor: Do you have physical symptoms like a racing heart, sweating, or trembling when you feel stressed or overwhelmed?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really get anything like that. I mean, I feel stressed out and overwhelmed a lot with the shop and everything, but my heart doesn't race, and I don't get sweaty or shaky. It's more just... mental stress, I guess you'd call it. Not physical.\n\nDoctor: Are you having any difficulty managing your personal care or daily activities at home due to your organizational issues?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Like I mentioned before, things at home are mostly fine. I'm still keeping up with my personal care and all the daily stuff around the house without any real issues. It's just the work and church stuff that's been a problem.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel mentally tired or exhausted after trying to focus on tasks or organize things?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I get tired at the end of the day like anyone else, but it's not like a mental exhaustion just from trying to focus. It's more that I just get frustrated or sidetracked, not necessarily worn out in my head.\n\nDoctor: Are your difficulties with organization and focus at work affecting your overall quality of life?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it really is. At the shop, it's... well, it's causing some real problems. We've had late orders, the billing gets confused, and that just leads to some pretty tense moments with the staff and the customers. And it's not just work, either. At church, I actually had to step back from leading some of the planning stuff we do, just because people were getting frustrated with me. So, yes, it's definitely taking a toll on things.\n\nDoctor: Are you noticing any strain in your relationships with friends or family members due to your current situation?\n\nPatient: No, not really. My family and friends have been pretty understanding, honestly. It's mostly just the work relationships and the church folks that have been strained because of the mistakes I've been making. But at home, things are okay.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or criticize yourself for the mistakes you've been making at work or with the church planning?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel guilty or beat myself up over it. I mean, I know I'm making mistakes, and it's frustrating when things go wrong at the shop or when I had to step down at church, but I don't really sit around criticizing myself. It is what it is, and I'm just trying to figure out how to handle it better.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced significant difficulties with attention or impulsivity in multiple settings, not just at work?\n\nPatient: Well, like I mentioned, it's definitely happening at work at the dry cleaners, and it was happening at church too before I stepped down from the planning committee. But other than those two places, I haven't really noticed it being a big issue anywhere else. At home, things are pretty normal, and I don't really have trouble when I'm just hanging out with my friends. So it's mostly just been work and church.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. And then I realize I completely missed half of what I was actually supposed to be checking.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I mean, I'm not depressed or anything like that. I get a little annoyed with myself sometimes when I mess up things at the shop, but as far as just feeling sad... no, my mood has been fine.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding certain tasks or situations at work because you feel overwhelmed by them?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really avoid things. I mean, I might put off doing the inventory for a bit because I know I'll get sidetracked, but I don't avoid tasks completely just because they're overwhelming. I still try to get everything done, even if it takes me longer than it should.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I know I get sidetracked, but I don't just... jump into things without thinking. I'm actually pretty careful about what I do, especially at the shop with people's clothes. I don't really make rash decisions or act out of the blue like that.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble managing your daily routines or responsibilities at home because of your difficulties with organization and focus?\n\nPatient: Well, at home it's mostly okay, but it's definitely causing trouble in other areas. Like at the shop, we've had late orders and... some confused billing lately. It's caused a few tense moments, honestly, with the staff and the customers. So, yeah, it is making my responsibilities harder to manage.\n\nDoctor: Was there anything significant happening in your life around the time your organizational issues started, about two years and two months ago?\n\nPatient: Yes, actually. That's exactly when I started changing the business into this more complicated boutique model. It's been causing real trouble for me ever since we made that shift, so... yeah, that's been about two years and two months now.\n\nDoctor: Do you have physical symptoms like a racing heart, sweating, or trembling when you feel stressed or overwhelmed?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really get anything like that. I mean, I feel stressed out and overwhelmed a lot with the shop and everything, but my heart doesn't race, and I don't get sweaty or shaky. It's more just... mental stress, I guess you'd call it. Not physical.\n\nDoctor: Are you having any difficulty managing your personal care or daily activities at home due to your organizational issues?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Like I mentioned before, things at home are mostly fine. I'm still keeping up with my personal care and all the daily stuff around the house without any real issues. It's just the work and church stuff that's been a problem.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel mentally tired or exhausted after trying to focus on tasks or organize things?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I get tired at the end of the day like anyone else, but it's not like a mental exhaustion just from trying to focus. It's more that I just get frustrated or sidetracked, not necessarily worn out in my head.\n\nDoctor: Are your difficulties with organization and focus at work affecting your overall quality of life?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it really is. At the shop, it's... well, it's causing some real problems. We've had late orders, the billing gets confused, and that just leads to some pretty tense moments with the staff and the customers. So, yes, it's definitely taking a toll on things.\n\nDoctor: Are you noticing any strain in your relationships with friends or family members due to your current situation?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It is what it is, and I'm just trying to figure out how to handle it better.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced significant difficulties with attention or impulsivity in multiple settings, not just at work?\n\nPatient: Well, like I mentioned, it's definitely happening at work at the dry cleaners, and it was happening at church too before I stepped down from the planning committee. But other than those two places, I haven't really noticed it being a big issue anywhere else. At home, things are pretty normal, and I don't really have trouble when I'm just hanging out with my friends. So it's mostly just been work and church.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced significant difficulties with attention or impulsivity since childhood?\n\nPatient: Yeah, actually. Even back in grade school, my teachers used to tell my mother that I was... well, they said I was bright, but just really scattered. I was always talking out of turn, or losing my papers. They constantly had to give me reminders just to sit and finish my work. So, it's definitely been going on since I was young.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.
